TELEGRAM CHAT TRANSCRIPT - YEAR 2021
================================================================================

Series          : one file per year, 2019, 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025, 2026 (this file is 2021)
Source          : result.json (Telegram Desktop JSON export)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
HOW TO READ THIS FILE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Days begin with:      ## DAY YYYY-MM-DD (Weekday)
Each message block:   [#<id>] HH:MM:SS <Display Name> (<user id>)
                      (annotation lines, zero or more, in parentheses)
                      <message text, may span several lines>
Blocks are separated by a blank line. Times are HH:MM:SS under the DAY
date above them, in the timezone the export was taken in.

#<id> is the Telegram message id. It is unique across the whole export and
increases with time, so replies can be traced across files by id.

Annotation lines you may see:
  (reply to #N - Author: "quoted snippet")   this message answers #N;
     a [YYYY-MM-DD] after the id means #N is from another day/file
  (forwarded from X) / (sent via bot @b) / (edited YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS)
  (attachment: ...)  media was posted; the binary is NOT part of this export
  (poll: "question" ...)                     a poll and its vote counts
  (reactions: EMOJI xN, ...)                 emoji reactions on the message
Lines starting with  * SERVICE  are membership events, not chat messages.

Author names are normalised: a given user id always appears under the same
display name (the one they used most often), so a person stays recognisable
even where the raw export recorded no name for a message.
Inline `code` and fenced ``` blocks preserve the original code formatting.

[#6718] 18:15:02 Unknown (user1672791186)
download audacity program for example

[#6719] 18:15:33 Unknown (user1672791186)
split the stereo track into two separate tracks

[#6720] 18:15:43 Unknown (user1672791186)
inverse the second one

[#6721] 18:16:23 Unknown (user1672791186)
then join them back to a stereo track and then mix down to mono

i think this is the one you looking for: A type 1 deterministic wallet is a simple method of generating addresses from a known starting string, as such it does not allow advanced features such as a Master Public Key. To generate a private key take SHA-256(string + n), where n is an ASCII-coded number that starts from 1 and increments as additional keys are needed.

This type of wallet can be created by Casascius Bitcoin Address Utility.

1. I guess they found out after the puzzle was made public [this alone adds unplanned and unnecessary confusion and for me it shows bad design]

2.  You can read all the public OP_data directly on the blockchain instead of going through whatever phase and whatever ciphers [this was also unplanned] How? Do something better with your time. Have fun, read and work hard. Be kind to others, it will pay off.

[#7958] 14:28:15 J (user332811965)
(attachment: photo 1280x166 49.0 KB [binary not included in export])

[#7959] 14:32:02 J (user332811965)
Frequency analysis on the first 91 characters (ending in 'abe') shows a disproportionate number of B's (and arguably also Es) relative to the other letters

[#7960] 14:33:03 J (user332811965)
(edited 2021-12-12 14:39:16)
I believe the B's serve as one kind of separator, and perhaps "BE" as another... "ABE" possibly an end-stop

[#7961] 14:40:50 J (user332811965)
I cannot immediately account for the double B's, or why there would be an occasional extra letter in the way of a successful matching of prime number spacing, but it seems too unlikely to be a coincidence

[#7962] 14:45:03 J (user332811965)
(attachment: photo 1280x189 33.6 KB [binary not included in export])

[#7963] 14:45:15 J (user332811965)
If you divide at the BE's

[#7964] 14:45:37 J (user332811965)
All the remaining sections are chunks consisting of prime numbers

[#7965] 14:46:14 J (user332811965)
Accounting for all primes 2-17

[#7966] 14:47:38 J (user332811965)
There is one double-BE section, so this separation ignores the first pair as a separator in order to have a grouping of 7

[#7967] 14:52:30 J (user332811965)
There are a few other patterns but these seem to me the most intentional

[#7968] 14:55:47 J (user332811965)
(edited 2021-12-12 14:56:00)
